Scope is `nextjs/` and `hyperwhisper-cloud/` TypeScript only.\n\n## Your trace unit: T7 — Stripe money-in (cluster C6) and the checkout that authors its metadata\n\n- **EP-10** `nextjs/app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:36` `POST` — **fully unauthenticated**. Body `amount` (validated `validateCreditPurchaseAmount`, `nextjs/app/api/checkout/credits/validation.ts:19`, integer 5..500), `licenseKey` (trimmed :63-67, DB lookup, `status !== \"granted\"` reject :106), `email` (regex :75, lowercased). Uses `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` (`nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:414`) keyed on `stripe_processed_events.stripe_object_id`, plus `getOrCreateUser`, `provisionAccountKeyForEmail`, `sent_emails`, Resend email templates.\n\n## What to walk\n\nRead both route handlers, `stripe-webhook.ts` in full, the `db-layer.ts` functions it calls, and the schemas `src/db/schema/stripe-processed-events.ts`, `account-keys.ts`, `credit-grants.ts`, `credit-balances.ts`, `sent-emails.ts`. Also read `nextjs/lib/clients/stripe.ts` and the email templates it triggers (`lib/templates/credit-mint-email.ts`, `credit-topup-email.ts`, `license-email.ts`, `lib/services/email.ts`).\n\nAt EVERY hop ask exactly one question: **\"What does this layer ASSUME that the layer above does not GUARANTEE?\"**\n\nFocus especially on:\n- **The metadata trust seam**: EP-10 is unauthenticated and authors `credit_amount`/`fee_cents`/`license_key`. Stripe faithfully echoes them back. Determine whether the webhook re-derives the credited amount from the AMOUNT ACTUALLY PAID (`amount_total`) or trusts `metadata.credit_amount`. If it trusts metadata, work out whether a caller who controls the checkout-session creation can get more credits than they pay for — and check every guard between EP-10's validator and the grant. Quote the exact lines.\n- **Idempotency across four paths**: `stripe_processed_events` is keyed on `stripe_object_id`. Determine which of top-up / mint / pool / refund actually insert that row, WHEN they insert it relative to the grant (before = lost grants on crash; after = double grants on retry), and how the 23505 unique-violation race is handled. Stripe retries webhooks — trace what a duplicate delivery does on each of the four paths.\n- **`checkout.session.completed` vs `async_payment_succeeded`**: can BOTH fire for one payment? If so, does the idempotency key cover both?\n- **The refund path** `handleChargeRefunded:473`: the partial-refund rule for credits (:518) vs full-only for licenses (:536). Work out what a partial refund of a mixed purchase does; whether `amount_refunded` is cumulative across multiple partial refunds (it is, in Stripe) and whether the code treats it as per-event; and whether repeated partial refunds can claw back more credits than were granted, or drive a balance negative.\n- **`parseInt` on `credit_amount`** (:190): what do `\"1e5\"`, `\"10.9\"`, `\" 12\"`, `undefined`, or an absent key produce, and does a `NaN` reach the DB or short-circuit?\n- **Ordering/partial failure**: grant → email → mark-processed. If the email send throws, is the grant rolled back or is the event left unmarked (and thus re-granted on Stripe's retry)? Check whether `sent_emails` dedup is the only thing preventing duplicate mail.\n- **EP-10's write to `stripeCustomerId`**: unauthenticated, keyed on a license key alone. Can a party who knows a license key re-point that row's `stripeCustomerId` to a customer they control, and what does that then unlock downstream (e.g. `customer.stripePortalUrl` in `nextjs/server/api/routers/customer.ts:189`)?\n- **Error contracts**: does the webhook return 2xx on internal failure (Stripe stops retrying → lost credits) or 5xx (Stripe retries → possible double)? Find every catch and what status it returns.\n\nHunt for: validation gaps reachable via a second path; tightened assumptions with unaware call sites; ordering/timing races; partial failure; concurrency (two webhook deliveries in flight); boundary mismatches (UNITS — cents vs dollars vs credits, numeric scale, string vs number) and ERROR CONTRACTS.\n\n## Return format\n\nFor each candidate bug return: `trace` (hops with file:line), `seam` (which hop pair breaks, naming both), `trigger_sketch` (the literal event payload / delivery sequence), `consequence` (user-visible failure — be concrete about money), `confidence` (low/medium/high).\nAlso return `depth_limits` and `files_opened`.\n\nDo NOT self-censor medium/low-confidence candidates. {"parentUuid":"3d72a7ef-c88d-4087-981e-ecadd489d3b8","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a370a3bfb6c20dc02","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. The\n14\t * license key IS the wallet, so this is the only way to obtain a key: a guest\n15\t * can buy credits with no key and the webhook mints + emails one. Topping up an\n16\t * existing key is the other path.\n17\t *\n18\t * REQUEST BODY:\n19\t * - amount: whole dollars, 5..500 (1000 credits per $1)\n20\t * - licenseKey (optional): when present, top up that key instead of minting\n21\t * - email (optional): mint path only. Prefills the Stripe checkout email so the\n22\t *   minted key is emailed to that address; ignored when topping up a key.\n23\t *\n24\t * PRICING (two non-bundled line items):\n25\t * - Credits: amount * 100 cents -> amount * 1000 credits\n26\t * - Processing fee (6%): round(amount * 100 * 0.06) cents — revenue, never\n27\t *   granted as credits, and non-refundable.\n28\t *\n29\t * STRIPE METADATA (read by the webhook):\n30\t * - purchase_type: \"credits\"\n31\t * - credit_amount: credits to grant\n32\t * - fee_cents: the processing fee charged\n33\t * - license_key: present ONLY when topping up an existing key (absent => mint)\n34\t */\n35\t\n36\texport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n37\t  try {\n38\t    const body = await req.json();\n39\t    const { licenseKey, amount, email } = body as {\n40\t      licenseKey?: unknown;\n41\t      amount?: unknown;\n42\t      email?: unknown;\n43\t    };\n44\t\n45\t    // Validate amount: whole dollars within [MIN, MAX].\n46\t    const amountError = validateCreditPurchaseAmount(amount);\n47\t    if (amountError !== null) {\n48\t      return NextResponse.json({ error: amountError }, { status: 400 });\n49\t    }\n50\t\n51\t    const { creditAmount, creditCents, feeCents } = computeCreditPurchase(\n52\t      amount as number\n53\t    );\n54\t\n55\t    const siteUrl =\n56\t      process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL || \"https://hyperwhisper.com\";\n57\t\n58\t    // Optional license key: when supplied, this is a top-up of an existing\n59\t    // wallet; when absent, the webhook mints a brand-new key on payment.\n60\t    // Trim first: a whitespace-only key must NOT count as \"present\" (which would\n61\t    // skip the mint path and then fail the key lookup). Normalize once and use the\n62\t    // trimmed value everywhere below.\n63\t    const normalizedLicenseKey =\n64\t      typeof licenseKey === \"string\" && licenseKey.trim().length > 0\n65\t        ? licenseKey.trim()\n66\t        : undefined;\n67\t    const hasLicenseKey = normalizedLicenseKey !== undefined;\n68\t\n69\t    // Mint path only: a guest-supplied email to prefill at checkout. Loose\n70\t    // format check — Stripe re-validates and the buyer can still edit it. The\n71\t    // webhook mints/emails the key off the email Stripe ultimately collects.\n72\t    const guestEmail =\n73\t      !hasLicenseKey &&\n74\t      typeof email === \"string\" &&\n75\t      /^[^\\s@]+@[^\\s@]+\\.[^\\s@]+$/.test(email.trim())\n76\t        ? email.trim().toLowerCase()\n77\t        : undefined;\n78\t\n79\t    const metadata: Record<string, string> = {\n80\t      purchase_type: \"credits\",\n81\t      credit_amount: creditAmount.toString(),\n82\t      fee_cents: feeCents.toString(),\n83\t    };\n84\t\n85\t    // Resolve the Stripe customer for the top-up path so credits attach to the\n86\t    // same customer/email. The mint path lets Stripe collect the email itself.\n87\t    let stripeCustomerId: string | undefined;\n88\t\n89\t    if (normalizedLicenseKey) {\n90\t      const license = await findAccountByKey(normalizedLicenseKey);\n91\t\n92\t      if (!license) {\n93\t        console.error(\n94\t          \"License lookup failed for key:\",\n95\t          normalizedLicenseKey.substring(0, 7)\n96\t        );\n97\t        return NextResponse.json(\n98\t          { error: \"Invalid license key\" },\n99\t          { status: 400 }\n100\t        );\n101\t      }\n102\t\n103\t      // Reject non-granted (e.g. revoked) licenses: credits added to them would\n104\t      // be unusable because /api/license/credits refuses to validate or deduct\n105\t      // them.\n106\t      if (license.status !== \"granted\") {\n107\t        return NextResponse.json(\n108\t          { error: `License is ${license.status}` },\n109\t          { status: 400 }\n110\t        );\n111\t      }\n112\t\n113\t      const email = license.email;\n114\t      if (!email) {\n115\t        return NextResponse.json(\n116\t          { error: \"License has no email associated\" },\n117\t          { status: 400 }\n118\t        );\n119\t      }\n120\t\n121\t      metadata.license_key = normalizedLicenseKey;\n122\t\n123\t      // Find or create the Stripe customer by email and cache it on the license.\n124\t      stripeCustomerId = license.stripeCustomerId ?? undefined;\n125\t      if (!stripeCustomerId) {\n126\t        const existingCustomers = await stripe.customers.list({\n127\t          email,\n128\t          limit: 1,\n129\t        });\n130\t\n131\t        if (existingCustomers.data.length > 0) {\n132\t          stripeCustomerId = existingCustomers.data[0].id;\n133\t        } else {\n134\t          const newCustomer = await stripe.customers.create({\n135\t            email,\n136\t            metadata: { license_key: normalizedLicenseKey },\n137\t          });\n138\t          stripeCustomerId = newCustomer.id;\n139\t        }\n140\t\n141\t        await updateAccountKey(license.id, { stripeCustomerId });\n142\t      }\n143\t    }\n144\t\n145\t    // Create Stripe Checkout Session\n146\t    // @ts-expect-error - managed_payments is in private preview\n147\t    const session = await stripe.checkout.sessions.create({\n148\t      mode: \"payment\",\n149\t      // Top-up: attach to the resolved customer. Mint: always create a customer\n150\t      // during checkout, prefilling the guest email when one was supplied.\n151\t      ...(stripeCustomerId\n152\t        ? { customer: stripeCustomerId }\n153\t        : {\n154\t            customer_creation: \"always\",\n155\t            ...(guestEmail ? { customer_email: guestEmail } : {}),\n156\t          }),\n157\t      line_items: [\n158\t        {\n159\t          price_data: {\n160\t            currency: \"usd\",\n161\t            product_data: {\n162\t              name: \"HyperWhisper Cloud credits\",\n163\t              description: `${creditAmount.toLocaleString()} credits`,\n164\t              // Managed Payments requires every line item's product to carry a\n165\t              // tax_code. txcd_10000000 = \"General - Electronically Supplied\n166\t              // Services\", which fits prepaid credits for a cloud STT service.\n167\t              tax_code: \"txcd_10000000\",\n168\t            },\n169\t            unit_amount: creditCents,\n170\t          },\n171\t          quantity: 1,\n172\t        },\n173\t        {\n174\t          price_data: {\n175\t            currency: \"usd\",\n176\t            product_data: {\n177\t              name: \"Cloud processing fee (6%)\",\n178\t              description: \"Non-refundable HyperWhisper Cloud processing fees\",\n179\t              // Same Managed Payments requirement: the fee line needs a tax_code.\n180\t              tax_code: \"txcd_10000000\",\n181\t            },\n182\t            unit_amount: feeCents,\n183\t          },\n184\t          quantity: 1,\n185\t        },\n186\t      ],\n187\t\n188\t      // Allow coupon/promotion codes\n189\t      allow_promotion_codes: true,\n190\t\n191\t      // Metadata for webhook processing\n192\t      metadata,\n193\t\n194\t      // Managed Payments: Stripe handles tax, invoicing, and compliance\n195\t      managed_payments: { enabled: true },\n196\t\n197\t      // Land on the public confirmation page (works for guests, who have no\n198\t      // account yet). It only personalizes copy from `credits` — entitlement is\n199\t      // always enforced server-side via the webhook-minted key.\n200\t      success_url: `${siteUrl}/purchase-success?session_id={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}&credits=${creditAmount}`,\n201\t      cancel_url: `${siteUrl}/credits`,\n202\t    });\n203\t\n204\t    // Return checkout URL to client\n205\t    if (session.url) {\n206\t      return NextResponse.json({\n207\t        checkoutUrl: session.url,\n208\t      });\n209\t    } else {\n210\t      throw new Error(\"No checkout URL returned from Stripe\");\n211\t    }\n212\t  } catch (error) {\n213\t    console.error(\"Credit checkout error:\", error);\n214\t\n215\t    return NextResponse.json(\n216\t      {\n217\t        error: \"Failed to create checkout session\",\n218\t        details: error instanceof Error ? error.message : \"Unknown error\",\n219\t      },\n220\t      { status: 500 }\n221\t    );\n222\t  }\n223\t}\n224\t"}]},"uuid":"cb6a9afb-de86-4a72-89e0-6393c80f91ab","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:40:17.831Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts","content":"import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\n\nimport { stripe } from \"@/lib/clients/stripe\";\nimport { findAccountByKey, updateAccountKey } from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\nimport {\n  validateCreditPurchaseAmount,\n  computeCreditPurchase,\n} from \"./validation\";\n\n/**\n * Stripe Checkout API Route for Credit Purchases\n *\n * Creates a Stripe Checkout Session for HyperWhisper credit purchases. The\n * license key IS the wallet, so this is the only way to obtain a key: a guest\n * can buy credits with no key and the webhook mints + emails one. Topping up an\n * existing key is the other path.\n *\n * REQUEST BODY:\n * - amount: whole dollars, 5..500 (1000 credits per $1)\n * - licenseKey (optional): when present, top up that key instead of minting\n * - email (optional): mint path only. Prefills the Stripe checkout email so the\n *   minted key is emailed to that address; ignored when topping up a key.\n *\n * PRICING (two non-bundled line items):\n * - Credits: amount * 100 cents -> amount * 1000 credits\n * - Processing fee (6%): round(amount * 100 * 0.06) cents — revenue, never\n *   granted as credits, and non-refundable.\n *\n * STRIPE METADATA (read by the webhook):\n * - purchase_type: \"credits\"\n * - credit_amount: credits to grant\n * - fee_cents: the processing fee charged\n * - license_key: present ONLY when topping up an existing key (absent => mint)\n */\n\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  try {\n    const body = await req.json();\n    const { licenseKey, amount, email } = body as {\n      licenseKey?: unknown;\n      amount?: unknown;\n      email?: unknown;\n    };\n\n    // Validate amount: whole dollars within [MIN, MAX].\n    const amountError = validateCreditPurchaseAmount(amount);\n    if (amountError !== null) {\n      return NextResponse.json({ error: amountError }, { status: 400 });\n    }\n\n    const { creditAmount, creditCents, feeCents } = computeCreditPurchase(\n      amount as number\n    );\n\n    const siteUrl =\n      process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL || \"https://hyperwhisper.com\";\n\n    // Optional license key: when supplied, this is a top-up of an existing\n    // wallet; when absent, the webhook mints a brand-new key on payment.\n    // Trim first: a whitespace-only key must NOT count as \"present\" (which would\n    // skip the mint path and then fail the key lookup). Normalize once and use the\n    // trimmed value everywhere below.\n    const normalizedLicenseKey =\n      typeof licenseKey === \"string\" && licenseKey.trim().length > 0\n        ? licenseKey.trim()\n        : undefined;\n    const hasLicenseKey = normalizedLicenseKey !== undefined;\n\n    // Mint path only: a guest-supplied email to prefill at checkout. Loose\n    // format check — Stripe re-validates and the buyer can still edit it. The\n    // webhook mints/emails the key off the email Stripe ultimately collects.\n    const guestEmail =\n      !hasLicenseKey &&\n      typeof email === \"string\" &&\n      /^[^\\s@]+@[^\\s@]+\\.[^\\s@]+$/.test(email.trim())\n        ? email.trim().toLowerCase()\n        : undefined;\n\n    const metadata: Record<string, string> = {\n      purchase_type: \"credits\",\n      credit_amount: creditAmount.toString(),\n      fee_cents: feeCents.toString(),\n    };\n\n    // Resolve the Stripe customer for the top-up path so credits attach to the\n    // same customer/email. The mint path lets Stripe collect the email itself.\n    let stripeCustomerId: string | undefined;\n\n    if (normalizedLicenseKey) {\n      const license = await findAccountByKey(normalizedLicenseKey);\n\n      if (!license) {\n        console.error(\n          \"License lookup failed for key:\",\n          normalizedLicenseKey.substring(0, 7)\n        );\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: \"Invalid license key\" },\n          { status: 400 }\n        );\n      }\n\n      // Reject non-granted (e.g. revoked) licenses: credits added to them would\n      // be unusable because /api/license/credits refuses to validate or deduct\n      // them.\n      if (license.status !== \"granted\") {\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: `License is ${license.status}` },\n          { status: 400 }\n        );\n      }\n\n      const email = license.email;\n      if (!email) {\n        return NextResponse.json(\n          { error: \"License has no email associated\" },\n          { status: 400 }\n        );\n      }\n\n      metadata.license_key = normalizedLicenseKey;\n\n      // Find or create the Stripe customer by email and cache it on the license.\n      stripeCustomerId = license.stripeCustomerId ?? undefined;\n      if (!stripeCustomerId) {\n        const existingCustomers = await stripe.customers.list({\n          email,\n          limit: 1,\n        });\n\n        if (existingCustomers.data.length > 0) {\n          stripeCustomerId = existingCustomers.data[0].id;\n        } else {\n          const newCustomer = await stripe.customers.create({\n            email,\n            metadata: { license_key: normalizedLicenseKey },\n          });\n          stripeCustomerId = newCustomer.id;\n        }\n\n        await updateAccountKey(license.id, { stripeCustomerId });\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Create Stripe Checkout Session\n    // @ts-expect-error - managed_payments is in private preview\n    const session = await stripe.checkout.sessions.create({\n      mode: \"payment\",\n      // Top-up: attach to the resolved customer. Mint: always create a customer\n      // during checkout, prefilling the guest email when one was supplied.\n      ...(stripeCustomerId\n        ? { customer: stripeCustomerId }\n        : {\n            customer_creation: \"always\",\n            ...(guestEmail ? { customer_email: guestEmail } : {}),\n          }),\n      line_items: [\n        {\n          price_data: {\n            currency: \"usd\",\n            product_data: {\n              name: \"HyperWhisper Cloud credits\",\n              description: `${creditAmount.toLocaleString()} credits`,\n              // Managed Payments requires every line item's product to carry a\n              // tax_code. txcd_10000000 = \"General - Electronically Supplied\n              // Services\", which fits prepaid credits for a cloud STT service.\n              tax_code: \"txcd_10000000\",\n            },\n            unit_amount: creditCents,\n          },\n          quantity: 1,\n        },\n        {\n          price_data: {\n            currency: \"usd\",\n            product_data: {\n              name: \"Cloud processing fee (6%)\",\n              description: \"Non-refundable HyperWhisper Cloud processing fees\",\n              // Same Managed Payments requirement: the fee line needs a tax_code.\n              tax_code: \"txcd_10000000\",\n            },\n            unit_amount: feeCents,\n          },\n          quantity: 1,\n        },\n      ],\n\n      // Allow coupon/promotion codes\n      allow_promotion_codes: true,\n\n      // Metadata for webhook processing\n      metadata,\n\n      // Managed Payments: Stripe handles tax, invoicing, and compliance\n      managed_payments: { enabled: true },\n\n      // Land on the public confirmation page (works for guests, who have no\n      // account yet). It only personalizes copy from `credits` — entitlement is\n      // always enforced server-side via the webhook-minted key.\n      success_url: `${siteUrl}/purchase-success?session_id={CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}&credits=${creditAmount}`,\n      cancel_url: `${siteUrl}/credits`,\n    });\n\n    // Return checkout URL to client\n    if (session.url) {\n      return NextResponse.json({\n        checkoutUrl: session.url,\n      });\n    } else {\n      throw new Error(\"No checkout URL returned from Stripe\");\n    }\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error(\"Credit checkout error:\", error);\n\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      {\n        error: \"Failed to create checkout session\",\n        details: error instanceof Error ? error.message : \"Unknown error\",\n      },\n      { status: 500 }\n    );\n  }\n}\n","numLines":224,"startLine":1,"totalLines":224}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"85f41a00-fea7-4034-a475-ee30240b3c56","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"3433c2c8-7c72-4587-9082-41e722c26f09","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"a370a3bfb6c20dc02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DqtY9gNaStRHj2qfnwSg2H","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Pure validation + pricing helpers for credit purchases.\n3\t *\n4\t * Kept free of Next/Stripe imports so they can be unit-tested directly (see\n5\t * tests/credit-purchase-validation.test.ts) and reused by the checkout route.\n6\t */\n7\t\n8\texport const MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS = 5;\n9\texport const MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS = 500;\n10\t/** 1,000 credits per US$1. */\n11\texport const CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR = 1000;\n12\t/** Non-refundable processing fee added on top of the credit value. */\n13\texport const CREDIT_FEE_RATE = 0.06;\n14\t\n15\t/**\n16\t * Validate the requested purchase amount (whole US dollars, MIN..MAX inclusive).\n17\t * Returns an error string, or null when valid.\n18\t */\n19\texport function validateCreditPurchaseAmount(amount: unknown): string | null {\n20\t  if (typeof amount !== \"number\" || !Number.isFinite(amount)) {\n21\t    return \"amount must be a finite number\";\n22\t  }\n23\t  if (!Number.isInteger(amount)) {\n24\t    return \"amount must be a whole number of dollars\";\n25\t  }\n26\t  if (amount < MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS || amount > MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS) {\n27\t    return `amount must be between ${MIN_CREDIT_DOLLARS} and ${MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS} dollars`;\n28\t  }\n29\t  return null;\n30\t}\n31\t\n32\texport interface CreditPurchaseBreakdown {\n33\t  /** Credits granted: amount * CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR. */\n34\t  creditAmount: number;\n35\t  /** Credit line-item Verifies webhook signature using STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET\n * - Uses raw body for signature verification (required by Stripe)\n */\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  const body = await try {\n    event = stripe.webhooks.constructEvent(body, signature, webhookSecret);\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\n      \"Stripe webhook signature verification failed:\",\n      err instanceof Error ? err.message : err\n    );\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Webhook signature verification failed\" },\n      { status: 400 }\n    );\n  }\n\n  console.log(`Stripe Stripe from \"stripe\";\n2\timport { stripe } from \"@/lib/clients/stripe\";\n3\timport { emailService } from \"@/lib/services/email\";\n4\timport { generateLicenseKey } from \"@/lib/services/license-key\";\n5\timport {\n6\t  findAccountByKey,\n7\t  getAccountKeysByEmail,\n8\t  findAccountByStripeSession,\n9\t  insertAccountKey,\n10\t  getOrCreateUser,\n11\t Handlers\n20\t *\n21\t * Service module for processing Stripe webhook events.\n22\t * Handles license purchases and credit purchases.\n23\t */\n24\t\n25\t/**\n26\t * Process a completed license purchase.\n27\t *\n28\t * CRITICAL: This function must be idempotent.\n29\t * Stripe may send the same event multiple times.\n30\t * We use upsert with stripe_session_id as the idempotency key.\n31\t */\n32\texport async function handleLicensePurchase(\n33\t  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session\n34\t): Promise<void> {\n35\t  const customerEmail = session.customer_details?.email;\n36\t  const customerName =\n37\t    session.customer_details?.name ||\n38\t    customerEmail?.split(\"@\")[0] ||\n39\t    \"Customer\";\n40\t  const stripeCustomerId = session.customer as string;\n41\t\n42\t  if (!customerEmail) {\n43\t    throw new Error(\"No customer email in checkout session\");\n44\t  }\n45\t\n46\t  console.log(`Processing license purchase for ${customerEmail}`);\n47\t\n48\t  // STEP 1: Check if we already processed this session (idempotency)\n49\t  const existingLicense = await findAccountByStripeSession(session.id);\n50\t\n51\t  if (existingLicense) {\n52\t    console.log(\n53\t      `License already exists for session ${session.id}, resending email...`\n54\t    );\n55\t    // Still send email in case it failed before\n56\t    await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, existingLicense.key);\n57\t    return;\n58\t  }\n59\t\n60\t  // STEP 2: Generate unique license key with collision check\n61\t  let licenseKey: string;\n62\t  let attempts = 0;\n63\t  const maxAttempts = 10;\n64\t\n65\t  do {\n66\t    licenseKey = generateLicenseKey();\n67\t    attempts++;\n68\t\n69\t    // Check uniqueness in database\n70\t    const collision = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n71\t\n72\t    if (!collision) break;\n73\t\n74\t    console.warn(`License key collision detected, retrying... (${attempts})`);\n75\t\n76\t    if (attempts >= maxAttempts) {\n77\t      throw new Error(\"Failed to generate unique license key after max attempts\");\n78\t    }\n79\t  } while (true);\n80\t\n81\t  console.log(`Generated license key: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n82\t\n83\t  // STEP 3: Get or create user for the customer\n84\t  const user = await getOrCreateUser(customerEmail, {\n85\t    name: customerName,\n86\t    stripeCustomerId,\n87\t  });\n88\t\n89\t  if (!user) {\n90\t    throw new Error(`Failed to create user for ${customerEmail}`);\n91\t  }\n92\t\n93\t  console.log(`User ready for ${customerEmail}: ${user.id}`);\n94\t\n95\t  // STEP 4: Store license in database\n96\t  let insertedLicense;\n97\t  try {\n98\t    insertedLicense = await insertAccountKey({\n99\t      key: licenseKey,\n100\t      email: customerEmail.toLowerCase().trim(),\n101\t      userId: user.id,\n102\t      stripeCustomerId,\n103\t      stripeSessionId: session.id,\n104\t      status: \"granted\",\n105\t    });\n106\t  } catch (insertError: unknown) {\n107\t    // Check if it's a duplicate (race condition with webhook retry)\n108\t    if (\n109\t      insertError &&\n110\t      typeof insertError === \"object\" &&\n111\t      \"code\" in insertError &&\n112\t      (insertError as { code: string }).code === \"23505\"\n113\t    ) {\n114\t      console.log(\"License already inserted by concurrent request\");\n115\t      return;\n116\t    }\n117\t    console.error(\"Failed to store license key:\", insertError);\n118\t    throw insertError;\n119\t  }\n120\t\n121\t  console.log(`License key stored in database for ${customerEmail}`);\n122\t\n123\t  // STEP 4b: Grant initial credits\n124\t  if (insertedLicense) {\n125\t    try {\n126\t      await grantCreditLot({\n127\t        userId: insertedLicense.userId,\n128\t        amount: 5000,\n129\t        sourceType: \"license_bundle\",\n130\t        sourceId: session.id,\n131\t      });\n132\t      console.log(`Granted 5000 initial credits for license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n133\t    } catch (creditError) {\n134\t      console.error(\"Failed to create initial credit balance:\", creditError);\n135\t      // Don't throw - license was created, credits can be added later\n136\t    }\n137\t  }\n138\t\n139\t  // STEP 5: Send license email\n140\t  await sendLicenseEmail(customerName, customerEmail, licenseKey);\n141\t}\n142\t\n143\t/**\n144\t * Send the license key email to the customer.\n145\t *\n146\t * Uses the existing email service with retry logic.\n147\t * Does not throw - logs errors but allows the webhook to succeed.\n148\t */\n149\tasync function sendLicenseEmail(\n150\t  customerName: string,\n151\t  customerEmail: string,\n152\t  licenseKey: string\n153\t): Promise<void> {\n154\t  const emailResult = await emailService.sendLicenseKey({\n155\t    customerName,\n156\t    customerEmail,\n157\t    licenseKey,\n158\t    productName: \"HyperWhisper\",\n159\t    downloadUrl: \"https://www.hyperwhisper.com\",\n160\t    supportEmail: \"support@hyperwhisper.com\",\n161\t  });\n162\t\n163\t  if (!emailResult.success) {\n164\t    // Log but don't throw - license is created, email can be resent manually\n165\t    console.error(`Failed to send license email: ${emailResult.error}`);\n166\t  } else {\n167\t    console.log(`License email sent to ${customerEmail}`);\n168\t  }\n169\t}\n170\t\n171\t/**\n172\t * Process a completed credit purchase.\n173\t *\n174\t * Two paths, decided by whether the checkout carried a license key:\n175\t * - TOP-UP (`metadata.license_key` present): add credits to the existing key.\n176\t * - MINT (`metadata.license_key` absent): a guest bought credits with no key,\n177\t *   so we mint one, grant the credits, and email the new key. This is now the\n178\t *   only way to obtain a key (the standalone license product is retired).\n179\t *\n180\t * CRITICAL: idempotent. Stripe may deliver the same event multiple times.\n181\t * Mint dedupes on license_keys.stripe_session_id; the grant dedupes on\n182\t * stripe_processed_events(stripe_object_id = session.id).\n183\t */\n184\texport async function handleCreditPurchase(\n185\t  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n186\t  eventId: string,\n187\t  eventType = \"checkout.session.completed\"\n188\t): Promise<void> {\n189\t  const licenseKey = session.metadata?.license_key;\n190\t  const creditAmount = parseInt(session.metadata?.credit_amount || \"0\", 10);\n191\t\n192\t  if (!creditAmount || creditAmount <= 0) {\n193\t    throw new Error(\n194\t      `Invalid credit amount in metadata: ${session.metadata?.credit_amount}`\n195\t    );\n196\t  }\n197\t\n198\t  if (session.payment_status !== \"paid\") {\n199\t    console.log(\n200\t      `Skipping credit purchase for session ${session.id}: payment_status=${session.payment_status}`\n201\t    );\n202\t    return;\n203\t  }\n204\t\n205\t  if (licenseKey) {\n206\t    await handleCreditTopUp(session, eventId, eventType, licenseKey, creditAmount);\n207\t  } else {\n208\t    await handleCreditMint(session, eventId, eventType, creditAmount);\n209\t  }\n210\t}\n211\t\n212\t/**\n213\t * Top-up path: grant credits to an existing license key and email a receipt.\n214\t */\n215\tasync function handleCreditTopUp(\n216\t  session: Stripe.Checkout.Session,\n217\t  eventId: string,\n218\t  eventType: string,\n219\t  licenseKey: string,\n220\t  creditAmount: number\n221\t): Promise<void> {\n222\t  console.log(\n223\t    `Processing credit top-up: ${creditAmount} credits for license ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`\n224\t  );\n225\t\n226\t  const license = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n227\t\n228\t  if (!license) {\n229\t    throw new Error(`License not found: ${licenseKey.substring(0, 7)}...`);\n230\t  }\n231\t\n232\t  // Refuse to credit a non-granted (e.g. revoked) license: those credits would be\n233\t  // unusable via /api/license/credits.
