{"parentUuid":"c72eea4c-aa8e-45d9-a0d7-4862491b4f7c","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a370a3bfb6c20dc02","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWHkX2dem3csfWEFGmj7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've traced the full T7 path. Findings below.\n\n---\n\n## T7 — Stripe money-in (C6) seam bugs\n\n### BUG-1 — Mint path: paid customer never receives their license key, and the retry is designed to *skip* the resend\n\n- **trace**\n  `nextjs/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:91` → `lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:184` `handleCreditPurchase` → `:294` `handleCreditMint` → `:389` `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` (commits event-row + grant in one tx, `src/lib/db-layer.ts:417-440`) → `:402` `if (grantResult === \"duplicate\") return;` → `:438` `emailService.sendCreditMint`\n- **seam**: `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` (atomic, idempotent, commits) ↔ `handleCreditMint`'s email step (non-transactional, non-idempotent, *and gated behind the same duplicate check*). The grant layer guarantees \"granted exactly once\"; the mint layer assumes that also means \"emailed exactly once\", but it only ever means \"emailed at most once\".\n- **trigger_sketch**\n  1. `evt_1` `checkout.session.completed`, `cs_test_A`, `metadata: {purchase_type:\"credits\", credit_amount:\"10000\", fee_cents:\"60\"}`, no `license_key`.\n  2. Grant tx commits (key minted, 10,000 credits attached).\n  3. `sendCreditMint` fails permanently — Resend 422 `validation_error` / suppressed recipient → `sendWithRetry` breaks immediately (`lib/services/email.ts:213`) and returns `{success:false}`; `:447` only `console.error`s. **Or**: the Vercel function is killed between the tx commit and the send (timeout/deploy), route returns nothing → Stripe retries.\n  4. Retry `evt_1'` → `findAccountByStripeSession` finds the key → `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` returns `\"duplicate\"` → `:402-407` **returns before the email**.\n- **consequence**: The buyer paid $5–$500, the row exists, credits exist, and the license key is in the DB — but the mint email is the *only* delivery channel. `app/[locale]/purchase-success/page.tsx` never renders the key (it only reads `credits` from the query string), there is no key-recovery/resend endpoint under `app/api/`, and the guest has no account. The money is taken and the product is undeliverable without a manual DB lookup by support.\n- **Note the asymmetry**: `handleLicensePurchase:51-58` explicitly *does* re-send on the already-processed path (\"Still send email in case it failed before\"). The newer credit-mint path deliberately inverted that (`:399-401`: \"this guard is what keeps a re-delivery from emailing twice\") and lost the recovery property. `sent_emails` (`src/db/schema/sent-emails.ts`) is an append-only audit log with **no unique index** — it is not a dedup mechanism, so the \"email twice\" fear it was protecting against was already unenforced anywhere.\n- **confidence**: high\n\n---\n\n### BUG-2 — Refund guard subtracts a *pre-tax* fee from a *tax-inclusive* charge total, so the intended policy-compliant credit refund never claws back credits\n\n- **trace**\n  `app/api/checkout/credits/route.ts:79-83` writes `fee_cents = round(amount*100*0.06)` (pre-tax cents) and `:195` `managed_payments: {enabled:true}` (Stripe computes and adds tax) → `lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:516-523`:\n  ```ts\n  const feeCents = parseInt(checkoutSession.metadata?.fee_cents || \"0\", 10);\n  const creditPortion = charge.amount - feeCents;\n  if (charge.amount_refunded < creditPortion) { ...return; }\n  ```\n- **seam**: EP-10's metadata author (fee in *pre-tax* cents) ↔ `handleChargeRefunded`'s threshold (`charge.amount` is the *tax-inclusive* captured total under Managed Payments). The refund layer assumes `charge.amount == creditCents + feeCents`; the checkout layer guarantees only `amount_total >= creditCents + feeCents`, with tax on top.\n- **trigger_sketch**: `amount: 10` → `creditCents 1000`, `feeCents 60`, session total with 20% VAT = `1272`. Merchant applies the documented policy at `:459-463` (\"refund only the credit value; the 6% fee is non-refundable\") and refunds the credit line plus its VAT: **$12.00 → `amount_refunded = 1200`**. Guard computes `creditPortion = 1272 - 60 = 1212`. `1200 < 1212` → `\"does not cover the credit portion … skipping\"`.\n- **consequence**: Customer receives $12.00 back **and keeps all 10,000 credits**. Every policy-compliant credit refund in a taxed jurisdiction silently no-ops; only a *full* refund (including the \"non-refundable\" fee) ever triggers the clawback — the exact opposite of the stated policy. Scales linearly: a $500 pack = $530 refunded and 500,000 credits retained.\n- **confidence**: medium-high (mechanically certain once tax > 0; Managed Payments exists precisely to add tax)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-3 — Credits are granted from `metadata.credit_amount`; `amount_total` is never read, and `allow_promotion_codes: true` is on\n\n- **trace**: `route.ts:189` `allow_promotion_codes: true` → `stripe-webhook.ts:190` `parseInt(session.metadata?.credit_amount || \"0\", 10)` → `:389` grant. I grepped the whole of `nextjs/{app,lib,src,server}`: **`amount_total` / `amount_subtotal` / `total_details` / `discounts` appear zero times.**\n- **seam**: EP-10 (authors `credit_amount` from the *list* price) ↔ EP-8 (grants `credit_amount` with no reference to what was actually captured). EP-8 assumes metadata still describes the money received; EP-10 guarantees only that it described the money *quoted*.\n- **trigger_sketch**: Any promotion code the merchant has ever created. `POST /api/checkout/credits {\"amount\":500}` → metadata `credit_amount:\"500000\"`, line items $500 + $30. Buyer enters a 90%-off code at checkout → `amount_total = 5300`. `payment_status: \"paid\"` → 500,000 credits granted for $53. With a **100%-off** coupon: `amount_total = 0`, `payment_status: \"paid\"`, **no charge object is created at all** — so 500,000 credits are granted for $0 and `charge.refunded` can never fire to claw them back.\n- **consequence**: The discount applies to the cash but not to the credits, and the code has no notion of \"credits earned per cent captured\". A single promo code is a credit-printing press bounded only by `MAX_CREDIT_DOLLARS`. The correct guard would be `creditAmount = Math.floor((amount_total - feeCents) * CREDITS_PER_DOLLAR / 100)`.\n- **confidence**: medium (certain mechanically; severity gated on whether any coupon exists in the Stripe account — but `allow_promotion_codes: true` is an explicit invitation)\n- **Related, same seam**: with a discount, `creditPortion = charge.amount - feeCents` (`:517`) can go **negative** (e.g. $500 + 95%-off → `2650 - 3000 = -350`), so `amount_refunded < creditPortion` is false for *any* refund — a $0.01 goodwill refund triggers a full 500,000-credit clawback.\n\n---\n\n### BUG-4 — Deliberate throws on the credit path are a poison pill: permanent 500s that Stripe retries for 3 days and that can disable the webhook endpoint\n\n- **trace**\n  `stripe-webhook.ts:192-196` (throw on bad `credit_amount`, *before* the payment_status check at `:198`) and `:235-239` (throw when `license.status !== \"granted\"`, with the comment \"Throw so the webhook returns non-2xx and the event surfaces for manual review\") → `route.ts:93-100` returns **500**.\n- **seam**: `handleCreditTopUp`'s error contract (\"non-2xx = a human will look at this\") ↔ the Stripe delivery layer's contract (\"non-2xx = redeliver, and sustained failure = disable the endpoint\"). The service layer assumes 5xx is an alerting channel; the transport layer guarantees it is a *retry* channel with an endpoint-level circuit breaker.\n- **trigger_sketch**: Customer tops up license `L`. Admin revokes `L` (support/chargeback flow) before or during the webhook retry window. `evt_topup` → `:235` throws → 500 → Stripe redelivers on backoff for 3 days, failing identically every time. Same for any legacy/dashboard-created session carrying `purchase_type:\"credits\"` without `credit_amount` (`:192`).\n- **consequence**: (a) the customer paid and got nothing, with no automated remediation; (b) Stripe's sustained-failure policy on the endpoint means *other, healthy* purchases can stop being delivered — every mint/top-up during the outage window silently receives no credits and no key. Contrast the refund path (`route.ts:126-129`), which does the opposite and swallows *everything* into a 200.\n- **confidence**: medium\n\n---\n\n### BUG-5 — Refund path returns 200 on every internal failure: a transient DB error permanently loses the clawback\n\n- **trace**: `route.ts:121-130`\n  ```ts\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error(\"Stripe webhook: Error processing refund:\", error);\n    // Don't return error status - log for manual review instead\n  }\n  ```\n  → falls through to `:132` `return NextResponse.json({ received: true })`.\n- **seam**: `handleChargeRefunded`/`refundCreditGrant` (throws on transient DB failure — pool exhaustion, `FOR UPDATE` deadlock at `db-layer.ts:465`/`:513`, Neon cold-start) ↔ the route's error contract, which converts *every* failure — transient or permanent — into a 200. `refundCreditGrant` assumes it will be re-invoked if it fails; the route guarantees it never will.\n- **trigger_sketch**: `charge.refunded` for `ch_X`. `refundCreditGrant` opens two `FOR UPDATE` scans over the user's grants and deadlocks against a concurrent `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` (same rows, `db-layer.ts:560-581`) — one side is aborted by Postgres. Route logs and returns 200. Stripe marks the event delivered and never retries.\n- **consequence**: Customer is refunded in cash and keeps 100% of the credits, permanently. Note this is *also* the only reason `refundCreditGrant`'s internal idempotency matters — and `handleCreditRefund` takes an `eventId` parameter it **never uses** (`:582`, no reference in the body), despite the docstrings at `:469-471` and `:575-577` claiming \"recorded in `stripe_processed_events` keyed by `charge.id`\". **No refund ever writes a `stripe_processed_events` row** (verified: `stripeProcessedEvents` is inserted in exactly one place, `db-layer.ts:418`). Idempotency happens to hold via `refunded_amount >= original_amount` (`:479`), but the documented mechanism does not exist.\n- **confidence**: high (contract), medium (frequency of trigger)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-6 — Partial refund below the credit portion claws back nothing (and is not cumulative-aware in the direction that matters)\n\n- **trace**: `stripe-webhook.ts:518` `if (charge.amount_refunded < creditPortion) return;` → then `handleCreditRefund:600` `refundCreditGrant(...)` claws back the **entire** `original_amount` (`db-layer.ts:478` `clawback = originalAmount - alreadyRefunded`), with no reference to the refunded *cash* amount.\n- **seam**: the threshold layer (reads cumulative `charge.amount_refunded`) ↔ the clawback layer (all-or-nothing on the grant). Neither layer implements proportionality, so the pair is a step function.\n- **trigger_sketch**: $100 pack (`credit_amount: \"100000\"`, `fee_cents: \"600\"`, `charge.amount = 10600`, `creditPortion = 10000`). Merchant issues a $50 goodwill refund → `amount_refunded = 5000 < 10000` → skip.\n- **consequence**: customer keeps 100,000 credits *and* $50. (Cumulativity does rescue the *repeated*-refund case: a second $50 refund pushes `amount_refunded` to 10000 and triggers the full clawback — so the failure is specifically the single sub-threshold partial refund, which is the common support action.) Conversely, once the threshold is crossed the clawback is always 100% regardless of how much cash was returned. I could not construct a case where repeated partials over-claw: `refundCreditGrant:479` `if (clawback <= 0) return duplicate` and `:489` `Math.min(clawback, getActiveGrantsTotal(...))` correctly prevent both double-clawback and a negative balance.\n- **confidence**: medium\n\n---\n\n### BUG-7 — EP-10 is unauthenticated, unthrottled, and turns a bare license key into a Stripe Checkout URL bound to the owner's Customer\n\n- **trace**: `middleware.ts:159` matcher `\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"` — **`/api` is excluded from middleware entirely**; `lib/rate-limit.ts` defines limiters only for `download-email` and `license-validate` — none for checkout. → `route.ts:90` `findAccountByKey` → `:126-141` `customers.list` / `customers.create` + `updateAccountKey(license.id, {stripeCustomerId})` → `:147` `sessions.create({customer: stripeCustomerId})` → `:206` returns `session.url` to the anonymous caller.\n- **seam**: `findAccountByKey` (a pure lookup — assumes its caller has already established that the requester is entitled to act on this key) ↔ EP-10, which treats *knowledge of the key string* as the entire authorization model, then performs a **write** and mints a customer-scoped Stripe URL.\n- **trigger_sketch**\n  ```\n  POST /api/checkout/credits\n  {\"amount\": 5, \"licenseKey\": \"<key>\"}\n  ```\n  → 200 `{checkoutUrl: \"https://checkout.stripe.com/c/pay/cs_live_...\"}` for a session with `customer: cus_...` of the key's owner. Also a clean 3-way oracle: `400 \"Invalid license key\"` / `400 \"License is <status>\"` / `200 + URL`.\n- **consequence**: (a) the returned Stripe-hosted page is bound to the owner's Customer and prefills/discloses the owner's **email address** (and, where the customer has saved payment methods, Stripe Checkout surfaces them) to anyone holding the key string — keys are pasted into support tickets, forums and app configs; (b) an unauthenticated write to `account_keys.stripeCustomerId`, which is exactly the field `server/api/routers/customer.ts:201` selects for `stripePortalUrl` (`licenses.find(l => l.stripeCustomerId)`) — the first caller to hit EP-10 for a key with a null `stripeCustomerId` decides, permanently, which Stripe Customer that user's billing portal opens (`:214-217`), since `:125` only ever writes when the field is empty and nothing re-resolves it; (c) unbounded anonymous `stripe.customers.create` + `checkout.sessions.create` with no rate limit, straight into Stripe API quota.\n  Note on (b): the customer is resolved from `license.email` (server-side, not attacker-controlled), so an attacker cannot directly point it at their own Customer. But `customers.list({email, limit: 1})` picks *one arbitrary* Customer among duplicates, and duplicate Customers with an arbitrary email are creatable by anyone via EP-10's own mint path (`customer_creation: \"always\"` + `customer_email: guestEmail`, `:154-155`) plus a small payment. That makes which Customer gets cached — and therefore which billing history the owner's portal shows — externally influenceable.\n- **confidence**: high for (c) and the enumeration oracle; medium for (a); low-medium for (b)\n\n---\n\n### BUG-8 — `handleCreditMint` pooling never records `stripeSessionId`, so a revocation mid-retry mints a silent orphan key\n\n- **trace**: `stripe-webhook.ts:319-332` pooling branch sets `license = existing` and **skips `insertAccountKey`**, so `account_keys.stripe_session_id` is never written for pooled purchases → on redelivery, `:312 findAccountByStripeSession(session.id)` returns null and the pooling lookup is re-run.\n- **seam**: `handleCreditMint`'s STEP 1 (two different resolution mechanisms — session-id and email-pooling — assumed to be equivalent and stable across deliveries) ↔ `account_keys.status`, which is mutable between deliveries.\n- **trigger_sketch**: `evt_1` pools $20 into granted key `K` (owned by `user@x.com`) and the grant commits. Admin revokes `K`. Stripe redelivers `evt_1` (or `async_payment_succeeded` for the same session). Now `findAccountByStripeSession` → null, and `existingKeys.find(k => k.status === \"granted\")` → undefined → a **brand-new key `K2`** is minted and inserted with `stripe_session_id = cs_X` → `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` returns `\"duplicate\"` (`stripe_object_id` already recorded) → `:402` returns before any email.\n- **consequence**: a phantom `granted` key exists that nobody was told about, carrying zero credits, while the paid credits sit on the revoked key `K` and are unspendable. Also makes `charge.refunded`'s license-path lookup (`findAccountByStripeSession`, `:546`) resolve to the wrong row for that session.\n- **confidence**: low-medium\n\n---\n\n### Checked and found sound (so the report isn't read as a whitelist)\n\n- **`checkout.session.completed` + `async_payment_succeeded` double-fire**: both call `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` with `stripeObjectId = session.id`, and the unique index is on `stripe_object_id` (`stripe-processed-events.ts:14-16`), not `event_id` — so the second event is correctly a `\"duplicate\"`. Covered.\n- **The 23505 race**: `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` uses `onConflictDoNothing({target: stripeObjectId})` inside a single transaction that also does the grant (`db-layer.ts:417-440`), so event-row and grant commit atomically. Mark-processed is *not* after the grant, and *not* in a separate tx. Two concurrent deliveries: one commits, the other's insert returns no row → `\"duplicate\"`. Correct.\n- **`parseInt` on `credit_amount`** (`:190`): `\" 12\"`→12, `\"10.9\"`→10, `\"1e5\"`→**1** (silent 100,000× under-grant), `undefined`→`\"0\"`→throws at `:192`. But EP-10 always writes `creditAmount.toString()` on an integer in 5,000–500,000, and JS only produces exponential notation ≥1e21, so no reachable trigger. `NaN` cannot reach the DB (`!creditAmount` catches it). Not a bug on this path — only a latent one if metadata ever gets another author.\n- **`refundCreditGrant` negative balance / over-clawback**: `Math.min(clawback, getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, user_id))` at `:489-492` plus the `FOR UPDATE` on both scans plus `reconcileCreditBalance` at `:547` genuinely prevent negatives and double-clawback. Solid.\n- **`credit_grants` numeric(20,2) vs JS `Number`**: all amounts are integers ≤ 500,000; `Number(row.remaining_amount)` round-trips exactly. No scale mismatch reachable here.\n\n---\n\n### depth_limits\n\n- Did not run the test suite (`tests/credit-purchase-validation.test.ts` covers only the pure validator; nothing in `tests/` exercises the webhook, the grant ledger, or the refund guard — **there is no test for `handleCreditPurchase`, `handleChargeRefunded`, or `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` at all**, which is why BUG-1/2/6 are unguarded).\n- BUG-2 and BUG-3 depend on runtime Stripe behavior I did not verify against the live account (whether Managed Payments makes `charge.amount` tax-inclusive; whether any promotion codes exist; whether `payment_status` is `\"paid\"` for a $0 session).
